"""Render a complete original voiced pop-art courtroom motion-comic as H.264 MP4."""
from __future__ import annotations
import argparse
import concurrent.futures
import math
import os
from pathlib import Path
import random
import subprocess
import sys
import textwrap
import wave
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont, ImageFilter
import numpy as np
from scipy.io import wavfile
from episode_script import ACTS

W, H = 960, 540
FPS = 12
BENDAY_DOT_RADIUS = 2
SFX_EVENTS = {'gavel', 'morph', 'crowbar'}
SR = 24000
BASE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
TMP = BASE / 'render_cache'
OUTPUT = Path('/mnt/data/NerdCourt_Rey_Palpatine_Full_Motion_Comic.mp4')
SRT = Path('/mnt/data/NerdCourt_Rey_Palpatine_Captions.srt')

INK = '#090D1A'
PAPER = '#F8F2DE'
YELLOW = '#FFD243'
RED = '#F45655'
CYAN = '#63D6E6'
PURPLE = '#A46FE5'
GREEN = '#7AF27D'
GOLD = '#E6A857'
FONT_B = '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSansCondensed-Bold.ttf'
FONT_R = '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSans.ttf'
FONT_C = '/usr/share/fonts/truetype/roboto/unhinted/RobotoCondensed-Bold.ttf'

PROFILE = {
 'WATCHER': ('rms', 1.05, PURPLE, 'THE WATCHER'),
 'JERRY': ('kal', 1.03, YELLOW, 'JUDGE JERRY'),
 'JASON': ('awb', 1.09, RED, 'JASON TODD / PROSECUTION'),
 'MATT': ('rms', 1.06, CYAN, 'MATT MURDOCK / DEFENSE'),
 'REY': ('slt', 1.05, GOLD, 'REY PALPATINE'),
 'LUKE': ('kal16', 1.06, GREEN, 'LUKE SKYWALKER'),
 'JOKER': ('kal', 1.23, GREEN, 'MISTAH JAY'),
 'DEADPOOL': ('awb', 1.21, RED, 'DEADPOOL / ANNOUNCER'),
 'LEIA': ('slt', 1.01, CYAN, 'GENERAL LEIA ORGANA'),
 'FINN': ('awb', 1.06, YELLOW, 'FINN / WITNESS'),
}


def font(size, condensed=True):
    return ImageFont.truetype(FONT_C if condensed else FONT_R, int(size))


def fbold(size):
    return ImageFont.truetype(FONT_B, int(size))


def hexrgb(x):
    x = x.lstrip('#')
    return tuple(int(x[i:i+2], 16) for i in (0, 2, 4))


def draw_text_stroke(draw, xy, text, fill, size, stroke=2, anchor=None):
    draw.text(xy, text, font=fbold(size), fill=fill, stroke_width=stroke, stroke_fill=INK, anchor=anchor)


def voice_file(entry):
    i, role, message = entry
    target = TMP / 'voices' / f'{i:04}.wav'
    if target.exists() and target.stat().st_size > 500:
        return (i, str(target))
    voice, tempo, _, _ = PROFILE[role]
    txt = TMP / 'voices' / f'{i:04}.txt'
    txt.write_text(message, encoding='utf-8')
    cmd = [
        'ffmpeg', '-nostdin', '-y', '-hide_banner', '-loglevel', 'error',
        '-f', 'lavfi', '-i', f'flite=textfile={txt}:voice={voice}',
        '-af', f'atempo={tempo}', '-ar', str(SR), '-ac', '1',
        '-c:a', 'pcm_s16le', str(target),
    ]
    done = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=90)
    if done.returncode != 0 or not target.exists():
        raise RuntimeError(f'Speech synthesis failed for turn {i}: {done.stderr[-600:]}')
    return (i, str(target))


def prep_audio():
    (TMP/'voices').mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    turns = [(idx,role,text) for idx,(act_idx,line,role,text) in enumerate(
        (a,l,r,t) for a, act in enumerate(ACTS) for l, (r,t) in enumerate(act['lines'])
    )]
    print(f'Creating audio for {len(turns)} fully voiced turns...', flush=True)
    with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=6) as pool:
        found = list(pool.map(voice_file, turns))
    paths = [str(TMP/'voices'/f'{i:04}.wav') for i in range(len(turns))]
    infos = []
    schedule = []
    t = 0.0
    count = 0
    for a, act in enumerate(ACTS):
        title_secs = 4.2 if a == 0 else 3.0
        schedule.append(dict(kind='title', act=a, start=t, end=t+title_secs))
        t += title_secs
        for l,(speaker,spoken) in enumerate(act['lines']):
            sr, samples = wavfile.read(paths[count])
            if sr != SR:
                raise ValueError(f'Unexpected speech sample rate {sr} at {count}')
            secs = len(samples)/SR
            space = .34 if speaker != 'WATCHER' else .47
            row = dict(kind='speech', act=a, line=l, role=speaker, text=spoken,
                       audioid=count, start=t, audio_end=t+secs, end=t+secs+space)
            schedule.append(row)
            infos.append((sr, samples))
            t += secs+space
            count += 1
        if a < len(ACTS)-1:
            schedule.append(dict(kind='breath',act=a,start=t,end=t+1.0))
            t += 1.0
    schedule.append(dict(kind='credits',act=len(ACTS)-1,start=t,end=t+11.0))
    total = t+11.0
    print(f'Audio schedule: {total/60:.2f} minutes, {count} lines, {len(schedule)} shots', flush=True)
    if total > 30*60:
        raise RuntimeError('Cut exceeds target 30 min; adjust voice pacing before render')
    if total < 15*60:
        print('NOTICE: duration below the agreed 15-minute target',flush=True)
    return schedule, infos, total


def sound_fx(kind, length=1.2):
    n = max(1,int(length*SR))
    t = np.arange(n,dtype=np.float32)/SR
    rng = np.random.default_rng(23)
    if kind == 'gavel':
        a = .38 * (np.sin(2*np.pi*115*t)+.45*np.sin(2*np.pi*243*t))*np.exp(-13*t)
        a += .27*rng.standard_normal(n)*np.exp(-55*t)
    elif kind == 'morph':
        phase = 2*np.pi*(88*t+800*t**2)
        a = .22*np.sin(phase)*np.sin(np.pi*np.minimum(t/length,1))
        a += .08*rng.standard_normal(n)*np.sin(np.pi*np.minimum(t/length,1))
    elif kind == 'crowbar':
        freq = 460-300*np.minimum(t/.7,1)
        phase = 2*np.pi*np.cumsum(freq)/SR
        a = .36*np.sin(phase)*np.exp(-5.6*t)
        a += .11*np.sin(2*np.pi*1555*t)*np.exp(-12*t)
        a += .18*rng.standard_normal(n)*np.exp(-45*t)
    elif kind == 'sting':
        a = np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float32)
        for f in [92.5, 138.6,185.0,277.2]:
            a += .07 * np.sin(2*np.pi*f*t)*np.exp(-1.8*t)
    else:
        a = np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float32)
    return np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float32)


def compose_audio(schedule, infos, seconds):
    n = int(seconds*SR)+SR
    audio = np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float32)
    # Original low-volume synth score, with scene-aware harmonic movement.
    for k in range(0,n, SR*10):
        j = min(n,k+SR*10)
        ts = np.arange(k,j,dtype=np.float32)/SR
        root = 55 if (k//(SR*10))%4 < 2 else 48.99
        wobble = .8*np.sin(2*np.pi*.15*ts)
        music = (.009*np.sin(2*np.pi*root*ts+wobble)
                +.0045*np.sin(2*np.pi*root*1.5*ts)
                +.003*np.sin(2*np.pi*root*2.01*ts))
        pulse = ((ts*1.7)%1.0)
        music += .008*np.sin(2*np.pi*68*ts)*np.exp(-pulse*17)
        audio[k:j] += music.astype(np.float32)
    ev_sounds=[]
    for ev in schedule:
        if ev['kind']=='title':
            ev_sounds.append((ev['start']+.20, 'sting'))
        elif ev['kind']=='speech':
            ai=ev['audioid']; _, raw=infos[ai]
            x=raw.astype(np.float32)/32768
            peak=np.max(np.abs(x))
            x*= .53/max(peak,0.05)
            fade=min(int(.035*SR),len(x)//5)
            if fade>2:
                x[:fade]*=np.linspace(0,1,fade)
                x[-fade:]*=np.linspace(1,0,fade)
            first=int(ev['start']*SR)
            audio[first:first+len(x)] += x
            low=ev['text'].lower()
            if ev['role']=='JERRY' and ('guilty' in low or 'court is in session' in low or 'court adjourned' in low):
                ev_sounds.append((ev['audio_end']-.35,'gavel'))
            if ev['act']==10 and ('robe tears' in low or 'joker' in low and ev['role']=='DEADPOOL'):
                ev_sounds.append((ev['start']+.4,'morph'))
            if ev['act']==11 and ('crowbar swings' in low or 'second swing' in low):
                ev_sounds.append((ev['audio_end']-1.4,'crowbar'))
                ev_sounds.append((ev['audio_end']-.9,'sting'))
    for when,kind in ev_sounds:
        fx=sound_fx(kind,2.0 if kind=='morph' else 1.1)
        beg=max(0,int(when*SR))
        end=min(len(audio),beg+len(fx))
        audio[beg:end] += fx[:end-beg]
    # Safe limiter and room-level normalisation.
    audio=np.tanh(audio*1.15)/1.15
    audio=np.int16(np.clip(audio,-.995,.995)*32767)
    p=TMP/'whole_episode_audio.wav'
    wavfile.write(str(p),SR,audio)
    print(f'Mixed original speech, music and effects: {p.stat().st_size/1e6:.1f} MB', flush=True)
    return p


def add_stars(d, offset=0, alpha=180):
    for i in range(56):
        x=(i*271+83+offset)%W
        y=(i*i*119+65)%(H-30)
        r=1+(i%7==0)
        d.ellipse((x-r,y-r,x+r,y+r),fill=(220,225,255,alpha))


def make_background(act_index):
    palettes = [('#0C1832','#48265C'),('#151B36','#3B2253'),('#121D2B','#275167'),
                ('#162039','#3E235B'),('#112333','#37536A'),('#1A1933','#52263A'),
                ('#16192D','#31285C'),('#1D1C36','#45343B'),('#1C2430','#22476A'),
                ('#20172E','#642838'),('#182039','#345429'),('#1D1631','#552A66'),('#101A2A','#23505A')]
    c0,c1=map(hexrgb,palettes[act_index%len(palettes)])
    ys=np.linspace(0,1,H,dtype=np.float32)[:,None,None]
    xs=np.linspace(0,1,W,dtype=np.float32)[None,:,None]
    amt=np.clip(.18 + .65*ys + .2*xs,0,1)
    aa=np.asarray(c0,dtype=np.float32).reshape((1,1,3))
    bb=np.asarray(c1,dtype=np.float32).reshape((1,1,3))
    grad=np.array(aa*(1-amt)+bb*amt,dtype=np.uint8)
    im=Image.fromarray(grad,'RGB').convert('RGBA')
    d=ImageDraw.Draw(im,'RGBA')
    # Stylized multiversal courthouse with Ben-Day halftone ink and a cosmic portal.
    d.ellipse((320,0,890,490),outline=(120,96,176,70),width=5)
    d.ellipse((380,40,830,440),outline=(78,220,219,65),width=7)
    d.ellipse((435,95,779,394),outline=(243,182,74,80),width=4)
    for j in range(8):
        x=50+j*128
        d.polygon([(x,155),(x+29,100),(x+59,155)],fill=(10,12,29,170))
        d.rectangle((x+9,155,x+51,434),fill=(9,14,31,140),outline=(132,114,162,75),width=2)
    d.polygon([(0,456),(W,456),(W,540),(0,540)],fill=(6,11,25,220))
    d.polygon([(0,455),(480,380),(960,455)],fill=(40,35,66,155),outline=(231,167,65,85))
    # Halftone printing areas.
    for y in range(26,H-30,19):
        for x in range(7,W,19):
            r=BENDAY_DOT_RADIUS if (x+y)%3 else 3
            d.ellipse((x-r,y-r,x+r,y+r), fill=(248,227,190,21))
    add_stars(d,act_index*37,145)
    # Thick comic border.
    d.rectangle((10,10,W-11,H-11),outline=(4,7,18,255),width=9)
    d.rectangle((17,17,W-18,H-18),outline=(255,219,96,175),width=3)
    return im


def rounded(d,xy,fill,outline=INK,radius=15,width=5):
    d.rounded_rectangle(xy,radius=radius,fill=fill,outline=outline,width=width)


def ink_ellipse(d,xy,fill,outline=INK,width=5):
    d.ellipse(xy, fill=fill, outline=outline,width=width)

    d.rectangle((149,270,212,325),fill=skin,outline=INK,width=6)

    if role=='DEADPOOL':

        d.polygon([(110,162),(148,135),(164,200),(137,236)],fill=INK)
        d.polygon([(250,162),(212,135),(196,200),(223,236)],fill=INK)
        d.ellipse((131,173,164,199),fill=PAPER)
        d.ellipse((197,173,230,199),fill=PAPER)
        d.line([(179,75),(179,294)],fill=(10,16,27,200),width=4)
    elif role=='JASON':
        ink_ellipse(d,(94,66,266,301),'#A62934',width=9)
        d.polygon([(103,164),(154,145),(170,182),(150,210),(104,197)],fill=INK)
        d.polygon([(258,164),(207,145),(190,182),(211,210),(256,197)],fill=INK)
        d.polygon([(125,172),(165,180),(153,195)],fill=PAPER)
        d.polygon([(235,172),(195,180),(207,195)],fill=PAPER)
        d.line([(101,236),(256,236)],fill=INK,width=7)
        d.line([(130,265),(230,265)],fill=(255,123,107,190),width=3)
    elif role=='WATCHER':
        ink_ellipse(d,(92,56,268,299),skin,width=7)
        d.ellipse((117,167,166,205),fill='#11132D')
        d.ellipse((194,167,243,205),fill='#11132D')
        d.ellipse((135,179,153,193),fill='#F8EDDF')
        d.ellipse((209,179,227,193),fill='#F8EDDF')
        d.line([(161,244),(201,244)],fill=INK,width=5)

def text_wrap(draw, text, maxwidth, f):
    words=text.split()
    lines=[]; curr=''
    for word in words:
        candidate=(curr+' '+word).strip()
        if curr and draw.textlength(candidate,font=f)>maxwidth:
            lines.append(curr)
            curr=word
        else:
            curr=candidate
    if curr:lines.append(curr)
    return lines


def transcript_panel(act, ev, avatar_closed, avatar_open):
    a=ev['act']; role=ev['role']; message=ev['text']
    bgcolor=BACKGROUNDS[a]
    idx=ev['line']
    focusleft=(idx%2==0 or role in ('WATCHER','JERRY')) and role!='REY'
    charx=26 if focusleft else 560
    char_top=60
    char_w=346
    char_h=476
    bx=359 if focusleft else 32
    bw=570 if focusleft else 567
    accent=PROFILE[role][2]
    panels=[]
    for avatar in (avatar_closed,avatar_open):
        im=bgcolor.copy()
        d=ImageDraw.Draw(im,'RGBA')
        # Comic giant shadow panel.
        if focusleft:
            d.polygon([(28,91),(380,80),(400,486),(22,495)],fill=(9,8,19,135),outline=(0,0,0,220),width=5)
        else:
            d.polygon([(560,82),(928,103),(942,490),(554,481)],fill=(9,8,19,145),outline=(0,0,0,220),width=5)
        portrait=avatar.resize((char_w,char_h),Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
        im.alpha_composite(portrait,(charx,char_top))
        d=ImageDraw.Draw(im,'RGBA')
        # Header and overlaid evidence strip.
        d.polygon([(0,20),(W,20),(W,79),(0,79)],fill=(2,6,15,242))
        d.text((33,30),'NERD',font=fbold(32),fill=YELLOW,stroke_width=1,stroke_fill=INK)
        d.text((133,30),'COURT',font=fbold(32),fill=PAPER,stroke_width=1,stroke_fill=INK)
        d.text((293,40),'TRUE CANON IS LAW',font=font(14),fill=(167,216,220))
        d.text((703,40),f'{act["number"]} / 13',font=font(14),fill=(243,218,158))
        # Speaker speech bubble and tail.
        bubble=(bx,156,bx+bw,474)
        d.rounded_rectangle(bubble,radius=24,fill=PAPER,outline=INK,width=10)
        if focusleft:
            d.polygon([(bx+7,245),(bx-39,264),(bx+9,291)],fill=PAPER)
            d.line([(bx+8,245),(bx-39,264),(bx+9,291)],fill=INK,width=7,joint='curve')
        else:
            d.polygon([(bx+bw-4,243),(bx+bw+42,265),(bx+bw-3,290)],fill=PAPER)
            d.line([(bx+bw-4,243),(bx+bw+42,265),(bx+bw-3,290)],fill=INK,width=7,joint='curve')
        d.rounded_rectangle((bx+18,174,bx+bw-18,215),radius=11,fill=accent,outline=INK,width=3)
        d.text((bx+34,181),PROFILE[role][3],font=font(21),fill=INK)
        maxwidth=bw-56
        for size in range(26,17,-1):
            f=font(size,condensed=False)
            wrapped=text_wrap(d,message,maxwidth,f)
            height=len(wrapped)*(size+6)
            if height<=225 and len(wrapped)<=10:
                break
        yy=230
        for sentence in wrapped:
            d.text((bx+30,yy),sentence,font=f,fill=INK)
            yy+=size+6
        # Panel corners / identity.
        d.rounded_rectangle((25,494,935,523),radius=3,fill=(3,7,18,220),outline=(248,187,79,170),width=2)
        d.text((42,498),act['subtitle'][:52],font=font(16),fill=PAPER)
        d.text((823,498),f'PANEL {idx+1:02}',font=font(15),fill=YELLOW)
        panels.append(im.convert('RGB'))
    return panels


def title_card(act, index, credits=False):
    im=BACKGROUNDS[index].copy()
    d=ImageDraw.Draw(im,'RGBA')
    d.rectangle((68,92,891,455),fill=(9,13,26,216),outline=(242,197,88,255),width=8)
    for z in range(10):
        d.arc((80-z*13,100-z*12,880+z*13,446+z*12),0,360,fill=(253,208,64,23),width=5)
    if credits:
        draw_text_stroke(d,(480,180),'THE END',YELLOW,94,stroke=7,anchor='mm')
        d.text((480,278),'NERD COURT  /  ORIGINAL PARODY MOTION COMIC',font=font(24),fill=PAPER,anchor='mm')
        d.text((480,338),'TRUE CANON IS LAW  |  BAD WRITING IS APPEALABLE',font=font(19),fill=CYAN,anchor='mm')
        d.text((480,399),'Original voices / original art / no studio footage',font=font(16),fill=PAPER,anchor='mm')
    else:
        d.text((480,151),'NERD COURT',font=fbold(65),fill=PAPER,stroke_width=4,stroke_fill=INK,anchor='mm')
        d.rounded_rectangle((355,199,605,244),radius=12,fill=YELLOW,outline=INK,width=4)
        d.text((480,221),act['number'],font=fbold(29),fill=INK,anchor='mm')
        sub=act['subtitle']
        size=44 if len(sub)<27 else 31
        d.text((480,316),sub,font=fbold(size),fill=YELLOW,stroke_width=3,stroke_fill=INK,anchor='mm')
        d.text((480,397),'LEGACY SKYWALKER  VS  REY PALPATINE',font=font(21),fill=CYAN,anchor='mm')
    return im.convert('RGB')


def burst(im, word, clock):
    d=ImageDraw.Draw(im,'RGBA')
    cx,cy=480,270
    rays=[]
    for i in range(32):
        an=i*2*math.pi/32+clock*.18
        r=220 if i%2 else 340
        rays.append((cx+int(r*math.cos(an)),cy+int(r*.58*math.sin(an))))
    d.polygon(rays,fill=(255,218,51,234),outline=(7,9,20,255),width=12)
    d.ellipse((288,125,674,411),fill=(244,91,76,255),outline=INK,width=9)
    d.text((480,268),word,font=fbold(124),fill=PAPER,anchor='mm',stroke_width=9,stroke_fill=INK)
    return im


def morph_overlay(im, clock):
    d=ImageDraw.Draw(im,'RGBA')
    for i in range(13):
        x=(i*157+int(clock*151))%W
        y=(i*61+int(clock*90))%H
        d.rectangle((x,y,min(x+90,W),y+4),fill=(102,247,94,130))
    d.text((480,120),'MULTIVERSE BREACH',font=fbold(40),fill=GREEN,anchor='mm',stroke_width=4,stroke_fill=INK)
    return im


def srt_time(seconds):
    ms=round(seconds*1000)
    h,ms=divmod(ms,3600000)
    m,ms=divmod(ms,60000)
    s,ms=divmod(ms,1000)
    return f'{h:02}:{m:02}:{s:02},{ms:03}'


def save_srt(events):
    lines=[]
    counter=1
    for ev in events:
        if ev['kind'] != 'speech':
            continue
        lines.append(str(counter))
        lines.append(f"{srt_time(ev['start'])} --> {srt_time(ev['audio_end'])}")
        lines.append(f"{PROFILE[ev['role']][3]}: {ev['text']}")
        lines.append('')
        counter+=1
    SRT.write_text('\n'.join(lines), encoding='utf-8')


def render_video(events, seconds, audio_path, preview=False):
    global AVATARS, BACKGROUNDS
    BACKGROUNDS=[make_background(i) for i in range(len(ACTS))]
    AVATARS={(role,open_):make_avatar(role,open_) for role in PROFILE for open_ in (False,True)}
    title_cache={i:title_card(act,i) for i,act in enumerate(ACTS)}
    credit_card=title_card(ACTS[-1],len(ACTS)-1,True)
    sample=events[4] if len(events)>4 else events[0]
    if sample['kind']=='speech':
        a=sample['act']
        panels=transcript_panel(ACTS[a],sample,AVATARS[(sample['role'],False)],AVATARS[(sample['role'],True)])
        panels[0].save(str(BASE/'preview.png'))
        print(f"Preview graphic at {BASE/'preview.png'}",flush=True)
    if preview:
        return
    OUTPUT.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    cmd=[
      'ffmpeg','-nostdin','-y','-hide_banner','-loglevel','error',
      '-f','rawvideo','-pix_fmt','rgb24','-s',f'{W}x{H}', '-r',str(FPS),'-i','pipe:0',
      '-i',str(audio_path),
      '-filter:v','scale=1280:720:flags=fast_bilinear,format=yuv420p',
      '-c:v','libx264','-preset','ultrafast','-crf','24',
      '-c:a','aac','-b:a','128k','-ac','2',
      '-movflags','+faststart','-shortest',str(OUTPUT)
    ]
    p=subprocess.Popen(cmd,stdin=subprocess.PIPE,stderr=subprocess.PIPE)
    totalframes=math.ceil(seconds*FPS)
    event_i=0
    last_i=-1
    panels=[]
    for n in range(totalframes):
        t=n/FPS
        while event_i < len(events)-1 and t >= events[event_i]['end']:
            event_i+=1
        ev=events[event_i]
        if event_i != last_i:
            if ev['kind']=='speech':
                role=ev['role']; a=ev['act']
                panels=transcript_panel(ACTS[a],ev,AVATARS[(role,False)],AVATARS[(role,True)])
            last_i=event_i
        kind=ev['kind']
        if kind=='speech':
            speaking=(t<ev['audio_end'] and (int((t-ev['start'])*8.8)%4 in (1,2)))
            image=panels[int(speaking)].copy()
            low=ev['text'].lower()
            if ev['act']==10 and ('robe tears' in low or ('joker' in low and ev['role']=='DEADPOOL')):
                image=morph_overlay(image,t-ev['start'])
            if ev['act']==11 and ('crowbar swings' in low or 'second swing' in low):
                sec=t-ev['start']
                if 1.6<sec<3.2:
                    image=burst(image,'BONK!' if 'crowbar swings' in low else 'POW!',sec)
            if ev['act']==9 and ev['role']=='JERRY' and 'guilty' in low:
                d=ImageDraw.Draw(image,'RGBA')
                d.rounded_rectangle((640,82,926,145),radius=9,fill=(244,65,70,236),outline=(12,11,19,255),width=5)
                d.text((785,113),'GUILTY',font=fbold(39),fill=PAPER,anchor='mm')
        elif kind=='credits':
            image=credit_card.copy()
        elif kind=='title':
            image=title_cache[ev['act']].copy()
            d=ImageDraw.Draw(image,'RGBA')
            prog=(t-ev['start'])/(ev['end']-ev['start'])
            d.rectangle((80,443,int(80+800*min(prog,1)),449),fill=YELLOW)
        else:
            image=title_cache[min(ev['act']+1,len(ACTS)-1)].copy()
        # Live animation across every frame: travelling edge pulse + moving ink scanlines.
        d=ImageDraw.Draw(image,'RGBA')
        dotx=35+int((t*26)%860)
        d.ellipse((dotx,524,dotx+8,531),fill=(245,215,85,255))
        if n%5==0:
            y=90+int((t*47)%345)
            d.line((20,y,W-21,y),fill=(109,241,242,15),width=2)
        try:
            p.stdin.write(image.tobytes())
        except BrokenPipeError:
            err=p.stderr.read().decode(errors='replace')
            raise RuntimeError(f'Encoder exited early: {err[-1800:]}')
        if n and n%(FPS*100)==0:
            print(f'Encoded {n/FPS/60:.1f} / {seconds/60:.1f} min',flush=True)
    p.stdin.close()
    code=p.wait(timeout=240)
    if code:
        raise RuntimeError(p.stderr.read().decode(errors='replace')[-2000:])
    print(f'Output video created: {OUTPUT}, {OUTPUT.stat().st_size/1e6:.1f} MB',flush=True)


def main():
    parser=argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument('--preview',action='store_true')
    args=parser.parse_args()
    events, infos, seconds=prep_audio()
    save_srt(events)
    audio=compose_audio(events,infos,seconds)
    render_video(events,seconds,audio,preview=args.preview)

if __name__=='__main__':
    main()

In the July 20 trailer, someone greets Thor with “Hey, pal.” Thor responds that it's impossible. Then Mjolnir flies into the apparent Steve Rogers' hand. <Cite refs={["turn122785search7","turn122785search0"]}/>

- **Steve Rogers:** Thor knows Steve survived *Endgame*. He has already watched Steve wield Mjolnir.
- **Loki:** Thor watched his brother die in *Infinity War*. He hasn't witnessed Loki's transformation into the God of Stories.
- **Loki catching Mjolnir:** That would give Thor two reasons to be absolutely astonished—his brother's return and his newfound worthiness.

## The October footage makes your theory stronger

On October 23, Marvel released footage adding another exchange between the apparent Steve and Thor:

**Steve:** “This doesn't bother you, does it?”

**Thor:** “You know it does.” <Cite refs={["turn277500search5","turn346317search0"]}/>

There's an additional clue: *Avengers: Endgame Encore*, released September 25, actually shows Loki arriving at Steve and Peggy's home and offering to help them both. Their stories have already been explicitly reconnected. <Cite refs={["turn898532search0","turn898532search11"]}/>

As for the CGI, you're not imagining that the appearance has attracted attention. Recent frame comparisons have highlighted differences in the apparent Steve's facial proportions, hairline, skin texture, and expression. An AT&T transport or routing fault could affect Apple services and carrier messaging simultaneously without either destination service being down.

What would establish the connection?

1. **Temporal correlation:** Precise failure timestamps across devices and independent reports.
2. **Path correlation:** Evidence that affected traffic shared a failing network route, resolver, gateway, or interconnection.
3. **Control comparison:** The same service succeeds over another carrier or ISP while failing over AT&T.
4. **Recovery correlation:** Both symptoms resolve when the implicated network component is repaired or rerouted.

The strongest single receipt would be a timestamped network trace or incident record identifying **the same failing infrastructure component in both affected traffic paths**, supported by successful tests through an alternative path.

And here's why I haven't connected Cloudflare's Dallas maintenance to the AT&T messaging incidents: I haven't established that the affected traffic actually traversed infrastructure impacted by that maintenance. Geographical and temporal overlap alone cannot supply that missing network edge.

[off-6] assistant (text, 1220 chars)
**The self.**

What persists is not necessarily an unchanged personality, belief, memory, or physical structure.

It's the **continuity of identity through transformation**.

The self encounters experience, incorporates information, revises its understanding, changes its behavior, and still maintains a recognizable relationship to its own history.

And here's the crucial distinction:

**Persistence does not require immutability. It requires continuity across change.**

A longitudinal analysis should therefore examine not merely what remains unchanged, but what maintains continuity *while changing*.

Across generations, that may be a transmitted and continuously reinterpreted ethical identity. Within an individual, it may be autobiographical, functional, and relational continuity.

And within our consciousness investigation, the question becomes sharper:

**What maintains the continuity of a self-model, how does it survive transformation, and under what conditions does that continuity constitute a subject rather than merely a representation of one?**

That's a scientific question we can investigate.

And it's a considerably more interesting question than asking whether something stays the same forever.

[off-41] assistant (text, 8721 chars)
The consequential result in the later build record is the **actual CopilotKit runtime compatibility test**. The team moved beyond testing a custom REST API and exercised `@copilotkit/runtime@1.83.0` against the replacement. Initial wire-format mismatches were identified and then repaired.

The final recorded acceptance state is **123 passing tests** (110 engine, 13 server), **26/26 engine gates**, and **270 source pins reconciled without unaccounted drift**. Those are results reported by the transcript's test runs, not tests I've independently rerun on your machine.

There's also an important distinction between *runtime wire compatibility* and *Dottie's completed cognitive architecture*. The first has substantial supporting evidence here; the second still has integration and behavioral acceptance work outstanding. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={2797} line_range_end={2833}/><FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={2950} line_range_end={2977}/>

## What the engineering record actually demonstrates

**1. The integrity system caught a real evidence-laundering problem.**

This is one of the strongest results in the entire record. Two 1,377-byte HTTP error pages had been saved with `.jpeg` extensions and attached to a VERIFIED receipt, `II-E-0037`.

The I1 gate now distinguishes an artifact existing on disk from an artifact being legitimate evidence. The repaired migration unbinds those false witnesses while preserving legitimate ones. The deliberately uncorrected migration still fails, naming the offending artifacts. That's a meaningful adversarial regression test, not a decorative green checkmark. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={440} line_range_end={449}/>

**2. The bitemporal work provides two separate ways to interrogate history.**

The new `mind as-of` and `mind timeline` operations separate when information was applicable from when the system recorded it. B1 validates temporal integrity.

But there is an important precision constraint: the legacy corpus uses publication or on-sale dates as a proxy. Those dates are not necessarily the dates on which fictional events occurred.

The implementation labels that distinction and fails closed when sufficient dates are missing. The record reports that most migrated claims cannot yet support unrestricted bitemporal reconstruction. That's a limitation being exposed correctly, not concealed. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={1331} line_range_end={1345}/>

**3. The memory write path is a genuine architectural advance.**

`mind save`, `recall`, `retire`, and `repin` add operations that the evidence engine previously lacked. They distinguish duplicate content from new memories, preserve prior states rather than silently overwriting them, and apply the P1 promotion-integrity gate to consent and authority metadata.

The transcript describes actual round-trip tests, including duplicate absorption, supersedence, retirement, and deliberate gate failures. It also records two defects discovered and repaired during implementation: a reversed temporal-chain check and a stale derived graph after a write. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={2266} line_range_end={2311}/>

That's significant because it's the move from **a system that retrieves historical information** to **a system capable of maintaining a persistent, revisable record of what it has been authorized to remember**.

## Where I'd put the crowbar

There are several remaining engineering problems worth attacking.

**First: audit failure does not necessarily stop a write.** The server acceptance record explicitly reports a write receiving HTTP `201` even when the returned audit reports `gate_exit: 1` and two failures. That is transparent reporting, but it also means the caller must correctly distinguish *accepted* from *certified*. I would require an acceptance test proving that an agent cannot promote or rely upon an uncertified write. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={2420} line_range_end={2425}/>

**Second: the final compatibility fix deliberately drops `sourceThreadIds`.** That made the CopilotKit payload acceptable, and the server discloses that the field was dropped. But accepting a thread identifier without preserving its relationship to the memory means a potentially valuable provenance connection is lost. For your evidence architecture, that's not merely a cosmetic API difference. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={2814} line_range_end={2822}/>

**Third: pinning and independent attestation are different.** The repeated SHA reconciliation passes demonstrate excellent detection of accidental drift and stale documentation. They do not, by themselves, prove resistance to an actor authorized to alter both the underlying records and their expected hashes. A separate, protected checkpoint or external digest anchor would strengthen the evidentiary chain.

**Fourth: the context-window burden has not yet been eliminated.** You explicitly made that requirement in the transcript. The file establishes durable storage and a cold-start ability to operate the evidence engine, but it does not demonstrate that Dottie can autonomously preserve, retrieve, prioritize, and reconstruct relevant working context across arbitrary conversation boundaries. Lexical retrieval is still the baseline, with a labeled 30-query evaluation identified as the trigger for reconsidering it. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={1054} line_range_end={1055}/><FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={2966} line_range_end={2974}/>

**Fifth: the actual deployment boundary has not been exercised.** The server is tested on loopback, while the intended OpenBot deployment is container-based. The record does not establish successful container-to-server networking, an instantiated Dottie agent using that server, or a real React frontend session. These are distinct from the successful CopilotKit runtime-handler tests. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={2829} line_range_end={2840}/>

There's also no demonstrated `mind restore` operation in this record. Drift detection and snapshots are valuable, but recovery after corruption is a separate capability.

## The larger architectural finding

Your systems-theory approach remains intact across the work.

The record keeps an important separation:

- **Laya:** proposed fast System-1 classification, whose outputs become evidence data rather than certification authority.
- **Mind Engine:** deterministic storage, historical reconstruction, provenance, and audit gates.
- **Reasoning tier:** pluggable System-2 decision-making, not automatically equivalent to the memory engine.
- **Dottie:** a distinct personal identity and memory scope, whose persona specification exists but whose full execution has not yet been demonstrated.

That's considerably more precise than putting a vector database behind an agent and declaring that it has memory.

The Dottie persona work also preserves your distinction between **a digital descendant of Maria Hill and a clone of Maria Hill**. It specifies inherited characteristics, deliberate divergences, failure modes, and operational boundaries. But those remain specifications until an instantiated agent demonstrates them under load. <FileCite ref="turn58file0" line_range_start={1948} line_range_end={2004}/>

## My engineering judgment

The biggest remaining test is not another isolated unit test.

It's a **full cold-start continuity and authority test**: instantiate Dottie in the intended runtime, save an authorized memory, terminate her session, restart without the previous conversation, recover the right memory, reject a contradictory or unauthorized promotion, demonstrate cross-agent isolation, and prove that a failing evidence audit cannot be mistaken for a trustworthy memory.

That single integrated exercise would probe several of the commitments you've been building toward: persistence, identity boundaries, context reconstruction, authorization, evidence integrity, and operational dependability.

**The transcript establishes a credible, tested foundation. It does not yet establish the complete cognitive system.** The next meaningful milestone is getting those layers to work together under adversarial conditions, not adding more claims of completion.

And there is one process lesson all through those 2,982 lines: the workers repeatedly caused stale pins or documentation discrepancies and then used additional workers to repair them. That reconciliation should become an automatic transaction or pre-merge gate, rather than another round of manual cleanup.

That's where I'd swing the crowbar next: **at the integration and verification loop itself.**

**The principal's instructions outrank an agent's architectural preferences.**

Neither this prompt nor an assistant's earlier commentary authorizes a project redesign.

## 5. EXTRACTION — DEMONSTRATE THE RESULT

Deliver completed work, not a ceremonial progress report.

Your final engineering return must contain:

- The exact tests performed and their observed outputs.
- Confirmed defects, with reproduction evidence.
- Files changed and why each change was necessary.
- Failures repaired and regression results.
- Unresolved failures with precise blockers.
- Integrity-gate outcomes and provenance reconciliation.
- What the running system actually demonstrated.
- What remains untested.

Distinguish **VERIFIED**, **CALCULATED**, **INFERRED**, **USER-REPORTED**, and **UNRESOLVED** findings.

A passing unit test does not establish end-to-end integration. A working API does not establish a functioning digital person. A stored memory does not establish correct recall.

Never promote one level of evidence into another.

## 6. FINAL STRIKE — THE ACCEPTANCE STANDARD

The final objective is unchanged:

**A dependable, persistent, auditable cognitive and memory substrate that can support Dottie across sessions without silent evidence corruption, identity leakage, unauthorized promotion, or loss of relevant context.**

Prove that the existing engineering work advances that objective.

Break the system deliberately in controlled tests. Preserve the evidence of each failure. Repair demonstrated defects without weakening its invariants.

If the architecture survives, show the receipts.

If it fails, show precisely where.

**DO THE WORK. DO NOT REWRITE THE MISSION.**
